#####################################################
# The data types used in scripts are:
# int:		Integer type
# bool:		Boolean type (true or false)
# string:	String
# function:	Function
# Card:		Card class
# Effect:	Effect class
# Group:	Card group class
# table:	Table type
# nil:		Empty type; also represents an empty variable
# any:		Any type
# For clarity, function signatures are written in a C-like style.
# [] means the parameter has a default value and can be omitted.
# nil parameters or return values are listed only when they are meaningful.
#
# Note: filter condition functions
# A filter condition function has a Card as its first parameter and returns a boolean. Filter APIs call this function repeatedly when building a group of cards that match a condition. Filter condition functions can accept extra parameters; parameters from the second one onward come from the filter API's extra arguments. Example:
# function sample_filter(c, atk, def)
# 	return c:GetAttack()>=atk and c:GetDefense()>=def
# end
# For a card c, this function returns true if its ATK is at least atk and its DEF is at least def; otherwise it returns false.
# g=Duel.GetFieldGroup(0,LOCATION_HAND,0) -- Get player 0's hand
# fg=g:Filter(sample_filter,nil,1000,500) -- Select cards from g with ATK >= 1000 and DEF >= 500
# Group.Filter needs only 3 fixed parameters. Arguments starting from the 4th are extra arguments and are passed to sample_filter.
#
# Note: specifying locations
# Some functions get cards from specified locations using three parameters: int player, int s, int o.
# s is the player's own locations from the perspective of player; o is the opponent's locations from the perspective of player.
# For example, Duel.GetFieldGroup(0,LOCATION_GRAVE,LOCATION_MZONE)
# returns all cards in player 0's Graveyard and player 1's Monster Zone.
#
# Note: descriptions
# The script system does not directly display prompt text from strings. Instead, it uses an integer (int desc) to look up the matching description.
# For desc < 2048, the system looks in string.conf. Some commonly used numbers, usually selection prompts, are recorded in constant.lua.
# For desc > 10000, the system looks in the card database. These descriptions are usually selected with aux.Stringid().
#
#●void Duel.ReplaceAttacker(Card c)
# Replaces the current attacking card with c during the Damage Step. This function no longer exists.
#●void Duel.ReplaceAttackTarget(Card c)
# Reserved; this function does not exist.
#
#
# Zone notes:
# On your field, Main Monster Zones are numbered 0~4 from left to right. Extra Monster Zones are 5~6. 0x7f means all Main Monster Zones plus Extra Monster Zones.
# Zone values are bit flags: 2 raised to the zone index. To recover the zone index, use math.log(zone,2).
# Opponent zone values are shifted left by 16 bits.
#
# Binary view:
#
# 0x7f = 0111 1111
# Reading from the low bit upward gives 1111 1110: the first 5 bits are your Main Monster Zones, the 6th bit is the left Extra Monster Zone, and the 7th bit is the right Extra Monster Zone.
# (0)= 1  = 0x1  = 0000 0001
# (1)= 2  = 0x2  = 0000 0010
# (2)= 4  = 0x4  = 0000 0100
# (3)= 8  = 0x8  = 0000 1000
# (4)= 16 = 0x10 = 0001 0000
# (5)= 32 = 0x20 = 0010 0000
# (6)= 64 = 0x40 = 0100 0000
#
#####################################################

●Effect|nil RushDuel.AddFusionProcedure(Card card, bool|any sub, bool|any insf, ... int|function|table)
Registers a Rush Fusion material procedure using fixed material slots. Each slot may be a card code, a predicate, or a table of alternatives. sub controls substitutes and insf permits non-Fusion special-summon handling; use explicit booleans to make the call unambiguous.
Parameters:
  card: Card - Fusion monster.
  sub: bool|any - Substitute permission; non-booleans are interpreted as the first material slot.
  insf: bool|any - Non-Fusion summon allowance; non-booleans are interpreted as a material slot.
  ...: int|function|table - Material slots.
Returns:
  Effect|nil - Fusion procedure effect; nil while copying effects.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDFusion.lua:15

●void RushDuel.AddMaximumProcedure(Card card, int max_atk, int left_code, int right_code)
Registers the center monster's Maximum Summon procedure, Maximum ATK and zone-limit effect. left_code and right_code identify the two components selected from hand. The pinned implementation stores components as overlays; use the matching Rush helpers when moving a Maximum monster.
Parameters:
  card: Card - Center Maximum monster.
  max_atk: int - ATK while in Maximum mode.
  left_code: int - Left component passcode.
  right_code: int - Right component passcode.

●void RushDuel.AttachAtkDef(Effect e, Card card, int|function|nil atk, int|function|nil def, [int reset], [bool forced])
Registers a single-card EFFECT_UPDATE_ATTACK effect for nonzero, non-nil atk, and an EFFECT_UPDATE_DEFENSE effect for nonzero, non-nil def only when RD.IsCanChangeDef(card) passes. The DEF check excludes Maximum-mode monsters; it does not block the ATK branch.
Both modifiers are created through RD.CreateSingleEffect with e:GetHandler() as owner, FLAG_ATTACH_EFFECT as label, and the supplied reset/forced values. No automatic duration is added when reset is omitted. Pass RESET_EVENT+RESETS_STANDARD+RESET_PHASE+PHASE_END for the usual until-End-Phase card modifier.
This helper returns no values and does not report whether the card's final stats changed. forced is passed to Card.RegisterEffect; leave it nil/false for normal effect-immunity handling.
Parameters:
  e: Effect - Resolving source effect; its handler owns the modifiers.
  card: Card - Card receiving the modifiers.
  atk: int|function|nil - ATK delta or value callback; nil and numeric 0 skip this branch.
  def: int|function|nil - DEF delta or value callback; nil and numeric 0 skip this branch, and RD.IsCanChangeDef must pass.
  reset: int optional - Explicit reset mask shared by both modifiers. Omitting it adds no reset policy.
  forced: bool optional - Passed to Card.RegisterEffect. Normally omit it rather than bypassing immunity.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDAttach.lua:5

●Group|nil RushDuel.CanSelect(any desc, any hint, any player, any group, any check, any min, any max, ... any)
Asks the player whether to select. Declining returns nil; canceling the card selection returns to the yes/no prompt. A completed selection returns its Group.
Parameters:
  desc: any
  hint: any
  player: any
  group: any
  check: any
  min: any
  max: any
  ...: any
Returns:
  Group|nil - Selected Group, or nil when the player declines.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDFunction.lua:45

●any RushDuel.CanSelectAndDoAction(int desc, int hint, function filter, int tp, int s_range, int o_range, int min, int max, Card|Group|nil expect, function action)
Optional resolution action: finds candidates, asks a yes/no question, then selects a Group and calls action(selected_group). Despite its name, this helper performs selection and the action; do not use it as a legality predicate or inside chk==0.
Declining the yes/no question, having too few candidates, or selecting an empty Group returns numeric 0 without running action. Canceling card selection returns to the yes/no question through RD.CanSelect. Once the player completes a nonempty selection, every callback return value is forwarded unchanged, including no values.
Like RD.SelectAndDoAction, it creates no activation target relation, passes no extra arguments to the filter/action, and emits a selection hint for public-location searches.
Parameters:
  desc: int - Yes/no question description identifier.
  hint: int - Card-selection prompt identifier.
  filter: function - Card predicate including required action-legality checks.
  tp: int - Selecting player; 0 or 1.
  s_range: int - Locations on tp's side.
  o_range: int - Locations on the opponent's side.
  min: int - Minimum selected count.
  max: int - Maximum selected count.
  expect: Card|Group|nil - Card(s) excluded from matching; pass nil for none.
  action: function - Called as action(selected_group) after the player accepts and completes selection.
Returns:
  any - Exactly the callback's return values when invoked; otherwise numeric 0. A callback may return no values.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDAction.lua:107

●function RushDuel.CostMerge(function cost1, function cost2)
Returns a cost callback that checks both costs with short-circuit 'and' when chk==0. During payment it calls both costs in order, ignoring their return values. Each cost must remain payable after the first payment; independently successful checks alone do not prove shared resources are sufficient.
Parameters:
  cost1: function - First cost callback.
  cost2: function - Second cost callback.
Returns:
  function - Combined cost callback.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDCost.lua:531

●function RushDuel.CostSendHandToGrave(function filter, int|function min, int|function max, [function|nil set_label_before], [function|nil set_object_before], [function|nil set_label_after], [function|nil set_object_after])
Returns a cost callback that selects min to max matching cards from the controller's hand and sends them to the Graveyard with REASON_COST, excluding the effect handler. The filter must include any required cost-legality check such as Card.IsAbleToGraveAsCost; this wrapper does not add that predicate.
The callback honors EFFECT_NO_COST_SEND_HAND_TO_GRAVE through RD.NoCostCheck. min and max can be numbers or callbacks evaluated with the cost arguments. Optional label callbacks receive the selected Group before sending or the operated Group after a successful send.
Parameters:
  filter: function - Card filter receiving card, effect, player and remaining cost callback arguments.
  min: int|function - Minimum count or callback computing it.
  max: int|function - Maximum count or callback computing it.
  set_label_before: function|nil optional - Computes the effect label from the selected Group before sending.
  set_object_before: function|nil optional - Computes the label object from the selected Group before sending.
  set_label_after: function|nil optional - Computes the effect label from the operated Group after a successful send.
  set_object_after: function|nil optional - Computes the label object from the operated Group after a successful send.
Returns:
  function - Cost callback for Effect.SetCost; tests availability for chk==0, then performs payment.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDCost.lua:299

●Effect RushDuel.CreateRitualEffect(any card, any type, any matfilter, any spfilter, any exfilter, any s_range, any o_range, any mat_check, any mat_move, any target_action, any operation_action, any limit_action, any including_self, any self_leave)
Creates an Effect with Rush Ritual target and operation callbacks. Configure the returned Effect's activation type, code, category and other requirements, then register it. The comparison mode is one of RITUAL_*; material locations default to zero and material movement defaults to RD.RitualToGrave.
Original/current selects which value of the Ritual monster sets the requirement. Material Level sums use Card.GetRitualLevel and ATK sums use Card.GetAttack in this snapshot; ORIGINAL does not universally mean reading every material's printed stat.
Parameters:
  card: any
  type: any
  matfilter: any
  spfilter: any
  exfilter: any
  s_range: any
  o_range: any
  mat_check: any
  mat_move: any
  target_action: any
  operation_action: any
  limit_action: any
  including_self: any
  self_leave: any
Returns:
  Effect - Configured but unregistered effect.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDRitual.lua:46

●int RushDuel.Draw([int player], [int count])
Draws with REASON_EFFECT using the supplied player/count, or the current chain's target player/parameter when arguments are omitted. Pair RD.TargetDraw(tp, count) in targeting with RD.Draw() in resolution.
Parameters:
  player: int optional - Player; defaults to CHAININFO_TARGET_PLAYER.
  count: int optional - Count; defaults to CHAININFO_TARGET_PARAM.
Returns:
  int - Result returned by Duel.Draw.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDTarget.lua:34

●void RushDuel.Init()
Initializes Rush rules, tracking flags and function overrides, and schedules the opening-turn setup. The pinned special.lua calls this through Auxiliary.PreloadUds; an MDPro3 integration may install its own bootstrap. This function is not guarded against repeated calls: do not call it from every card script.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDRule.lua:10

●Group|nil RushDuel.Select(int hint, int player, Group group, function|nil check, bool cancelable, int min, int max, ... any)
Displays the supplied selection hint, then selects cards from group. Without check it uses Select or CancelableSelect; with check it uses SelectSubGroup. Always guard a cancelable result before dereferencing it.
Parameters:
  hint: int - Selection hint identifier.
  player: int - Selecting player, 0 or 1.
  group: Group - Candidate cards.
  check: function|nil - Optional whole-group predicate; pass nil for an ordinary selection.
  cancelable: bool - Whether cancellation is permitted.
  min: int - Minimum selected count.
  max: int - Maximum selected count.
  ...: any
Returns:
  Group|nil - Selected cards; nil on cancellation.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDFunction.lua:32

●any RushDuel.SelectAndDoAction(int hint, function filter, int tp, int s_range, int o_range, int min, int max, Card|Group|nil expect, function action)
Finds matching cards in the specified locations, asks tp to select a non-cancelable Group of min to max cards, and immediately calls action(selected_group). Use during resolution, not during a chk==0 legality test. This helper does not select activation targets or store a target relation.
The public wrapper passes only a Card to filter and only the selected Group to action; capture other values in closures or compose a filter with RD.Filter. It emits a selection hint for public-location searches. If there are too few candidates or the selected Group is empty, it returns numeric 0 without calling action.
On a nonempty selection it forwards every return value from action unchanged. A callback with no return makes this helper return no values (nil in a single-value assignment); it does not automatically return the selected count or success.
Parameters:
  hint: int - Selection prompt identifier, such as HINTMSG_TOGRAVE or aux.Stringid(id, n).
  filter: function - Card predicate. Include the needed operation-legality checks.
  tp: int - Selecting player; 0 or 1.
  s_range: int - Locations on tp's side.
  o_range: int - Locations on the opponent's side.
  min: int - Minimum selected count.
  max: int - Maximum selected count.
  expect: Card|Group|nil - Card(s) excluded from matching; pass nil for no exclusion. The upstream parameter is spelled expect.
  action: function - Called as action(selected_group) immediately after a nonempty selection.
Returns:
  any - Exactly the callback's return values when invoked; otherwise numeric 0. A callback may return no values.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDAction.lua:102

●any|nil RushDuel.SelectOption(int player, ... table)
Accepts option tables {available, description, value}. Unavailable options are omitted; a sole option is announced automatically. Returns the chosen value (or its original one-based option index), not Duel.SelectOption's zero-based menu index.
The default is implemented as value or index: both nil and false use the original one-based index, while numeric 0 is preserved because it is truthy in Lua. With no available options this helper returns nil.
Parameters:
  player: int - Selecting player, 0 or 1.
  ...: table - Each option is {available, description, value}; value may be omitted.
Returns:
  any|nil - Chosen option value; nil when no options are available.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDFunction.lua:59

#####################################################
# The data types used in scripts are:
# int:		Integer type
# bool:		Boolean type (true or false)
# string:	String
# function:	Function
# Card:		Card class
# Effect:	Effect class
# Group:	Card group class
# table:	Table type
# nil:		Empty type; also represents an empty variable
# any:		Any type
# For clarity, function signatures are written in a C-like style.
# [] means the parameter has a default value and can be omitted.
# nil parameters or return values are listed only when they are meaningful.
#
# Note: filter condition functions
# A filter condition function has a Card as its first parameter and returns a boolean. Filter APIs call this function repeatedly when building a group of cards that match a condition. Filter condition functions can accept extra parameters; parameters from the second one onward come from the filter API's extra arguments. Example:
# function sample_filter(c, atk, def)
# 	return c:GetAttack()>=atk and c:GetDefense()>=def
# end
# For a card c, this function returns true if its ATK is at least atk and its DEF is at least def; otherwise it returns false.
# g=Duel.GetFieldGroup(0,LOCATION_HAND,0) -- Get player 0's hand
# fg=g:Filter(sample_filter,nil,1000,500) -- Select cards from g with ATK >= 1000 and DEF >= 500
# Group.Filter needs only 3 fixed parameters. Arguments starting from the 4th are extra arguments and are passed to sample_filter.
#
# Note: specifying locations
# Some functions get cards from specified locations using three parameters: int player, int s, int o.
# s is the player's own locations from the perspective of player; o is the opponent's locations from the perspective of player.
# For example, Duel.GetFieldGroup(0,LOCATION_GRAVE,LOCATION_MZONE)
# returns all cards in player 0's Graveyard and player 1's Monster Zone.
#
# Note: descriptions
# The script system does not directly display prompt text from strings. Instead, it uses an integer (int desc) to look up the matching description.
# For desc < 2048, the system looks in string.conf. Some commonly used numbers, usually selection prompts, are recorded in constant.lua.
# For desc > 10000, the system looks in the card database. These descriptions are usually selected with aux.Stringid().
#
#●void Duel.ReplaceAttacker(Card c)
# Replaces the current attacking card with c during the Damage Step. This function no longer exists.
#●void Duel.ReplaceAttackTarget(Card c)
# Reserved; this function does not exist.
#
#
# Zone notes:
# On your field, Main Monster Zones are numbered 0~4 from left to right. Extra Monster Zones are 5~6. 0x7f means all Main Monster Zones plus Extra Monster Zones.
# Zone values are bit flags: 2 raised to the zone index. To recover the zone index, use math.log(zone,2).
# Opponent zone values are shifted left by 16 bits.
#
# Binary view:
#
# 0x7f = 0111 1111
# Reading from the low bit upward gives 1111 1110: the first 5 bits are your Main Monster Zones, the 6th bit is the left Extra Monster Zone, and the 7th bit is the right Extra Monster Zone.
# (0)= 1  = 0x1  = 0000 0001
# (1)= 2  = 0x2  = 0000 0010
# (2)= 4  = 0x4  = 0000 0100
# (3)= 8  = 0x8  = 0000 1000
# (4)= 16 = 0x10 = 0001 0000
# (5)= 32 = 0x20 = 0010 0000
# (6)= 64 = 0x40 = 0100 0000
#
#####################################################

# Format kept compatible with DataEditorX CodeConfig.AddFunction().

●Effect|nil RD.AddFusionProcedure(Card card, bool|any sub, bool|any insf, ... int|function|table)
Registers a Rush Fusion material procedure using fixed material slots. Each slot may be a card code, a predicate, or a table of alternatives. sub controls substitutes and insf permits non-Fusion special-summon handling; use explicit booleans to make the call unambiguous.
Parameters:
  card: Card - Fusion monster.
  sub: bool|any - Substitute permission; non-booleans are interpreted as the first material slot.
  insf: bool|any - Non-Fusion summon allowance; non-booleans are interpreted as a material slot.
  ...: int|function|table - Material slots.
Returns:

●void RD.AttachAtkDef(Effect e, Card card, int|function|nil atk, int|function|nil def, [int reset], [bool forced])
Registers a single-card EFFECT_UPDATE_ATTACK effect for nonzero, non-nil atk, and an EFFECT_UPDATE_DEFENSE effect for nonzero, non-nil def only when RD.IsCanChangeDef(card) passes. The DEF check excludes Maximum-mode monsters; it does not block the ATK branch.
Both modifiers are created through RD.CreateSingleEffect with e:GetHandler() as owner, FLAG_ATTACH_EFFECT as label, and the supplied reset/forced values. No automatic duration is added when reset is omitted. Pass RESET_EVENT+RESETS_STANDARD+RESET_PHASE+PHASE_END for the usual until-End-Phase card modifier.
This helper returns no values and does not report whether the card's final stats changed. forced is passed to Card.RegisterEffect; leave it nil/false for normal effect-immunity handling.
Parameters:
  e: Effect - Resolving source effect; its handler owns the modifiers.
  card: Card - Card receiving the modifiers.
  atk: int|function|nil - ATK delta or value callback; nil and numeric 0 skip this branch.
  def: int|function|nil - DEF delta or value callback; nil and numeric 0 skip this branch, and RD.IsCanChangeDef must pass.
  reset: int optional - Explicit reset mask shared by both modifiers. Omitting it adds no reset policy.
  forced: bool optional - Passed to Card.RegisterEffect. Normally omit it rather than bypassing immunity.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDAttach.lua:5

●Group|nil RD.CanSelect(any desc, any hint, any player, any group, any check, any min, any max, ... any)
Asks the player whether to select. Declining returns nil; canceling the card selection returns to the yes/no prompt. A completed selection returns its Group.
Parameters:
  desc: any
  hint: any
  player: any
  group: any
  check: any
  min: any
  max: any
  ...: any
Returns:
  Group|nil - Selected Group, or nil when the player declines.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDFunction.lua:45

●any RD.CanSelectAndDoAction(int desc, int hint, function filter, int tp, int s_range, int o_range, int min, int max, Card|Group|nil expect, function action)
Optional resolution action: finds candidates, asks a yes/no question, then selects a Group and calls action(selected_group). Despite its name, this helper performs selection and the action; do not use it as a legality predicate or inside chk==0.
Declining the yes/no question, having too few candidates, or selecting an empty Group returns numeric 0 without running action. Canceling card selection returns to the yes/no question through RD.CanSelect. Once the player completes a nonempty selection, every callback return value is forwarded unchanged, including no values.
Like RD.SelectAndDoAction, it creates no activation target relation, passes no extra arguments to the filter/action, and emits a selection hint for public-location searches.
Parameters:
  desc: int - Yes/no question description identifier.
  hint: int - Card-selection prompt identifier.
  filter: function - Card predicate including required action-legality checks.
  tp: int - Selecting player; 0 or 1.
  s_range: int - Locations on tp's side.
  o_range: int - Locations on the opponent's side.
  min: int - Minimum selected count.
  max: int - Maximum selected count.
  expect: Card|Group|nil - Card(s) excluded from matching; pass nil for none.
  action: function - Called as action(selected_group) after the player accepts and completes selection.
Returns:
  any - Exactly the callback's return values when invoked; otherwise numeric 0. A callback may return no values.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDAction.lua:107

●function RD.CostMerge(function cost1, function cost2)
Returns a cost callback that checks both costs with short-circuit 'and' when chk==0. During payment it calls both costs in order, ignoring their return values. Each cost must remain payable after the first payment; independently successful checks alone do not prove shared resources are sufficient.
Parameters:
  cost1: function - First cost callback.
  cost2: function - Second cost callback.
Returns:
  function - Combined cost callback.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDCost.lua:531

●function RD.CostSendHandToGrave(function filter, int|function min, int|function max, [function|nil set_label_before], [function|nil set_object_before], [function|nil set_label_after], [function|nil set_object_after])
Returns a cost callback that selects min to max matching cards from the controller's hand and sends them to the Graveyard with REASON_COST, excluding the effect handler. The filter must include any required cost-legality check such as Card.IsAbleToGraveAsCost; this wrapper does not add that predicate.
The callback honors EFFECT_NO_COST_SEND_HAND_TO_GRAVE through RD.NoCostCheck. min and max can be numbers or callbacks evaluated with the cost arguments. Optional label callbacks receive the selected Group before sending or the operated Group after a successful send.
Parameters:
  filter: function - Card filter receiving card, effect, player and remaining cost callback arguments.
  min: int|function - Minimum count or callback computing it.
  max: int|function - Maximum count or callback computing it.
  set_label_before: function|nil optional - Computes the effect label from the selected Group before sending.
  set_object_before: function|nil optional - Computes the label object from the selected Group before sending.
  set_label_after: function|nil optional - Computes the effect label from the operated Group after a successful send.
  set_object_after: function|nil optional - Computes the label object from the operated Group after a successful send.
Returns:
  function - Cost callback for Effect.SetCost; tests availability for chk==0, then performs payment.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDCost.lua:299

●Effect RD.CreateRitualEffect(any card, any type, any matfilter, any spfilter, any exfilter, any s_range, any o_range, any mat_check, any mat_move, any target_action, any operation_action, any limit_action, any including_self, any self_leave)
Creates an Effect with Rush Ritual target and operation callbacks. Configure the returned Effect's activation type, code, category and other requirements, then register it. The comparison mode is one of RITUAL_*; material locations default to zero and material movement defaults to RD.RitualToGrave.
Original/current selects which value of the Ritual monster sets the requirement. Material Level sums use Card.GetRitualLevel and ATK sums use Card.GetAttack in this snapshot; ORIGINAL does not universally mean reading every material's printed stat.
Parameters:

  type: any
  matfilter: any
  spfilter: any
  exfilter: any
  s_range: any
  o_range: any

  mat_move: any

●int RD.Draw([int player], [int count])

●void RD.Init()
Initializes Rush rules, tracking flags and function overrides, and schedules the opening-turn setup. The pinned special.lua calls this through Auxiliary.PreloadUds; an MDPro3 integration may install its own bootstrap. This function is not guarded against repeated calls: do not call it from every card script.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDRule.lua:10

●function RD.NoCostCheck(function cost, int flag_effect)
Wraps a cost callback with a Rush cost-waiver check. An applicable player effect with the specified code waives the cost when its value is 1 or its value callback returns true; otherwise the original cost callback receives all arguments.
Parameters:
  cost: function - Cost callback to invoke when no waiver applies.
  flag_effect: int - Player-effect code that can waive this cost.

  function - Wrapped cost callback.

●Group|nil RD.Select(int hint, int player, Group group, function|nil check, bool cancelable, int min, int max, ... any)
Displays the supplied selection hint, then selects cards from group. Without check it uses Select or CancelableSelect; with check it uses SelectSubGroup. Always guard a cancelable result before dereferencing it.
Parameters:
  hint: int - Selection hint identifier.
  player: int - Selecting player, 0 or 1.
  group: Group - Candidate cards.
  check: function|nil - Optional whole-group predicate; pass nil for an ordinary selection.
  cancelable: bool - Whether cancellation is permitted.
  min: int - Minimum selected count.
  max: int - Maximum selected count.
  ...: any
Returns:
  Group|nil - Selected cards; nil on cancellation.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDFunction.lua:32

●any RD.SelectAndDoAction(int hint, function filter, int tp, int s_range, int o_range, int min, int max, Card|Group|nil expect, function action)
Finds matching cards in the specified locations, asks tp to select a non-cancelable Group of min to max cards, and immediately calls action(selected_group). Use during resolution, not during a chk==0 legality test. This helper does not select activation targets or store a target relation.
The public wrapper passes only a Card to filter and only the selected Group to action; capture other values in closures or compose a filter with RD.Filter. It emits a selection hint for public-location searches. If there are too few candidates or the selected Group is empty, it returns numeric 0 without calling action.
On a nonempty selection it forwards every return value from action unchanged. A callback with no return makes this helper return no values (nil in a single-value assignment); it does not automatically return the selected count or success.
Parameters:
  hint: int - Selection prompt identifier, such as HINTMSG_TOGRAVE or aux.Stringid(id, n).
  filter: function - Card predicate. Include the needed operation-legality checks.
  tp: int - Selecting player; 0 or 1.
  s_range: int - Locations on tp's side.
  o_range: int - Locations on the opponent's side.
  min: int - Minimum selected count.
  max: int - Maximum selected count.
  expect: Card|Group|nil - Card(s) excluded from matching; pass nil for no exclusion. The upstream parameter is spelled expect.
  action: function - Called as action(selected_group) immediately after a nonempty selection.
Returns:
  any - Exactly the callback's return values when invoked; otherwise numeric 0. A callback may return no values.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDAction.lua:102

●any|nil RD.SelectOption(int player, ... table)
Accepts option tables {available, description, value}. Unavailable options are omitted; a sole option is announced automatically. Returns the chosen value (or its original one-based option index), not Duel.SelectOption's zero-based menu index.
The default is implemented as value or index: both nil and false use the original one-based index, while numeric 0 is preserved because it is truthy in Lua. With no available options this helper returns nil.
Parameters:
  player: int - Selecting player, 0 or 1.
  ...: table - Each option is {available, description, value}; value may be omitted.
Returns:
  any|nil - Chosen option value; nil when no options are available.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDFunction.lua:59

●Group RD.ToGroup(Group|Card|Effect target)
Creates a new Group from a Group, Card or Effect (using the Effect handler). Unsupported input produces an empty Group.
Parameters:
  target: Group|Card|Effect - Object to convert.
Returns:
  Group - New Group containing the supplied cards or handler.
Source: Rush/Utilities/RDFunction.lua:98

●Group RD.ToMaximunGroup(Group|Card|Effect target)
Converts the input to a Group and includes overlay components of cards satisfying RD.IsMaximumMode. The upstream name is spelled ToMaximunGroup; preserve that spelling.
Parameters:
  target: Group|Card|Effect - Object to convert and expand.
Returns:
  Group - Group including Maximum components.
